> **系列定位**：本文是「部署格式专题」之一 —— Intel 平台（x86 CPU / 核显 / NPU）。内容为 Ultralytics 官方英文文档完整归档，与中文实操文章互补。总目录见 [README 导读](README.md)。

class="group relative">Intel OpenVINO Export<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#intel-openvino-export">#</a></h1>
<img alt="OpenVINO Intel AI inference toolkit" decoding="async" loading="lazy" src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/openvino-ecosystem.avif" width="1024"/>
<p>In this guide, we cover exporting YOLO26 models to the <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">OpenVINO</a> format, which can provide up to 3x <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/cpu-device.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">CPU</a> speedup, as well as accelerating YOLO inference on Intel <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/gpu-device.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">GPU</a> and <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/npu-device.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">NPU</a> hardware.</p>
<p>OpenVINO, short for Open Visual Inference &amp; <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Neural Network</a> Optimization toolkit, is a comprehensive toolkit for optimizing and deploying AI inference models. Even though the name contains Visual, OpenVINO also supports various additional tasks including language, audio, time series, etc.</p>
<div align="center">
  <br/>
  <iframe allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" allowFullScreen="" class="aspect-video w-full border-0" loading="lazy" src="https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/AvFh-oTGDaw" title="YouTube video player" frameBorder="0">
  </iframe>
  <br/>
  <strong>Watch:</strong> How to Export Ultralytics YOLO26 to Intel OpenVINO Format for Faster Inference 🚀
</div>
<h2 id="supported-tasks" class="group relative">Supported Tasks<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#supported-tasks">#</a></h2>
<p>OpenVINO export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.</p>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Task</th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolov8">YOLOv8</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo11">YOLO11</a></th><th style="text-align:left"><a href="/models/yolo26">YOLO26</a></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/detect">Detect</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/segment">Segment</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/semantic">Semantic</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/depth">Depth</a></td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">❌</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/classify">Classify</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/pose">Pose</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><a href="/tasks/obb">OBB</a></td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td><td style="text-align:left">✅</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<h2 id="usage-examples" class="group relative">Usage Examples<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#usage-examples">#</a></h2>
<p>The OpenVINO format supports the <a href="/modes/export">Export</a>, <a href="/modes/predict">Predict</a>, and <a href="/modes/val">Validate</a> modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy on Intel CPU, integrated/discrete GPU, or NPU.</p><section class="admon admon-example"><div # Export the model to OpenVINO format

# Load the exported OpenVINO model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n_openvino_model/&quot;)

# Run inference

results = model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;)

# Run inference on a specific device, available devices: [&quot;intel:gpu&quot;, &quot;intel:npu&quot;, &quot;intel:cpu&quot;]

results = model(&quot;https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg&quot;, device=&quot;intel:gpu&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><section class="admon admon-example"><div ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load the exported OpenVINO model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n_openvino_model/&quot;)

# Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset

metrics = model.val(data=&quot;coco8.yaml&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><h2 id="export-arguments" class="group relative">Export Arguments<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#export-arguments">#</a></h2>

<div class="md-table"><table><thead><tr><th>Argument</th><th>Type</th><th>Default</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>format</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>&#x27;openvino&#x27;</code></td><td>Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.</td></tr><tr><td><code>imgsz</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>tuple</code></td><td><code>640</code></td><td>Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple <code>(height, width)</code> for specific dimensions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>quantize</code></td><td><code>int</code> or <code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Quantization precision: <code>16</code> (FP16) or <code>8</code> (INT8/PTQ; needs calibration <code>data</code>/<code>fraction</code>); <code>32</code>/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated <code>half</code>/<code>int8</code> flags.</td></tr><tr><td><code>dynamic</code></td><td><code>bool</code></td><td><code>False</code></td><td>Allows dynamic input sizes, enhancing flexibility in handling varying image dimensions.</td></tr><tr><td><code>nms</code></td><td><code>bool</code></td><td><code>False</code></td><td>Adds Non-Maximum Suppression (NMS), essential for accurate and efficient detection post-processing.</td></tr><tr><td><code>batch</code></td><td><code>int</code></td><td><code>1</code></td><td>Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in <code>predict</code> mode.</td></tr><tr><td><code>data</code></td><td><code>str</code></td><td><code>None</code></td><td>Path to the <a href="/datasets">dataset</a> YAML, essential for quantization; classification instead takes a dataset directory or a built-in dataset name. If omitted with <code>quantize=8</code>, Ultralytics selects the default calibration dataset for the model task.</td></tr><tr><td><code>fraction</code></td><td><code>float</code></td><td><code>1.0</code></td><td>Specifies the fraction of the dataset to use for INT8 quantization calibration. Allows for calibrating on a subset of the full dataset, useful for experiments or when resources are limited. If not specified with INT8 enabled, the full dataset will be used.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>For more details about the export process, visit the <a href="/modes/export">Ultralytics documentation page on exporting</a>.</p><section class="admon admon-warning"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-triangle-alert admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m21.73 18-8-14a2 2 0 0 0-3.48 0l-8 14A2 2 0 0 0 4 21h16a2 2 0 0 0 1.73-3"></path><path d="M12 9v4"></path><path d="M12 17h.01"></path></svg><span>Warning</span></div><div class="admon-content"><p>OpenVINO™ is compatible with most Intel® processors but to ensure optimal performance:</p>
<ol>
<li>
<p>Verify OpenVINO™ support
Check whether your Intel® chip is officially supported by OpenVINO™ using <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/about-openvino/release-notes-openvino/system-requirements.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Intel&#x27;s compatibility list</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Identify your accelerator
Determine if your processor includes an integrated NPU (Neural Processing Unit) or GPU (integrated GPU) by consulting <a href="https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/support/articles/000097597/processors.html" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Intel&#x27;s hardware guide</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Install the latest drivers
If your chip supports an NPU or GPU but OpenVINO™ isn&#x27;t detecting it, you may need to install or update the associated drivers. Follow the <a href="https://medium.com/openvino-toolkit/how-to-run-openvino-on-a-linux-ai-pc-52083ce14a98" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">driver‑installation instructions</a> to enable full acceleration.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<p>By following these three steps, you can ensure OpenVINO™ runs optimally on your Intel® hardware.</p></div></section><h2 id="benefits-of-openvino" class="group relative">Benefits of OpenVINO<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#benefits-of-openvino">#</a></h2>
<ol>
<li><strong>Performance</strong>: OpenVINO delivers high-performance inference by utilizing the power of Intel CPUs, integrated and discrete GPUs, and FPGAs.</li>
<li><strong>Support for Heterogeneous Execution</strong>: OpenVINO provides an API to write once and deploy on any supported Intel hardware (CPU, GPU, FPGA, VPU, etc.).</li>
<li><strong>Model Optimizer</strong>: OpenVINO provides a Model Optimizer that imports, converts, and optimizes models from popular <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">deep learning</a> frameworks such as PyTorch, <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">TensorFlow</a>, TensorFlow Lite, Keras, ONNX, PaddlePaddle, and Caffe.</li>
<li><strong>Ease of Use</strong>: The toolkit comes with a large collection of <a href="https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">tutorial notebooks</a> (including <a href="https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/tree/latest/notebooks/yolov26-optimization" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26 optimization</a>) teaching different aspects of the toolkit.</li>
</ol>
<h2 id="openvino-export-structure" class="group relative">OpenVINO Export Structure<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#openvino-export-structure">#</a></h2>
<p>When you export a model to OpenVINO format, it results in a directory containing the following:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>XML file</strong>: Describes the network topology.</li>
<li><strong>BIN file</strong>: Contains the weights and biases binary data.</li>
<li><strong>Mapping file</strong>: Holds mapping of original model output tensors to OpenVINO tensor names.</li>
</ol>
<p>You can use these files to run inference with the OpenVINO Inference Engine.</p>
<h2 id="using-openvino-export-in-deployment" class="group relative">Using OpenVINO Export in Deployment<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#using-openvino-export-in-deployment">#</a></h2>
<p>Once your model is successfully exported to the OpenVINO format, you have two primary options for running inference:</p>
<ol>
<li>
<p>Use the <code>ultralytics</code> package, which provides a high-level API and wraps the OpenVINO Runtime.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Use the native <code>openvino</code> package for more advanced or customized control over inference behavior.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<h3 id="inference-with-ultralytics" class="group relative">Inference with Ultralytics<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#inference-with-ultralytics">#</a></h3>
<p>The ultralytics package allows you to easily run inference using the exported OpenVINO model via the predict method. You can also specify the target device (e.g., <code>intel:gpu</code>, <code>intel:npu</code>, <code>intel:cpu</code>) using the device argument.</p>
<div class="code-block"><button type="button" tabindex="0" data-slot="tooltip-trigger" aria-label="Copy to clipboard" id="base-ui-_R_3sn79d9inpampfivb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" class="group/button inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center border border-transparent bg-clip-padding text-sm font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-all outline-none select-none focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50 active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-50 aria-invalid:border-destructive aria-invalid:ring-3 aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20 dark:aria-invalid:border-destructive/50 dark:aria-invalid:ring-destructive/40 [&amp;_svg]:pointer-events-none [&amp;_svg]:shrink-0 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-foreground hover:bg-muted hover:text-foreground aria-expanded:bg-muted aria-expanded:text-foreground dark:hover:bg-muted/50 size-7 rounded-[min(var(--radius-md),12px)] in-data-[slot=button-group]:rounded-lg code-copy"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-4 w-4" aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load the exported OpenVINO model

ov_model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n_openvino_model/&quot;)  # the path of your exported OpenVINO model

# Run inference with the exported model

ov_model.predict(device=&quot;intel:gpu&quot;)  # specify the device you want to run inference on</code></pre></div>
<p>This approach is ideal for fast prototyping or deployment when you don&#x27;t need full control over the inference pipeline.</p>
<h3 id="inference-with-openvino-runtime" class="group relative">Inference with OpenVINO Runtime<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#inference-with-openvino-runtime">#</a></h3>
<p>The OpenVINO Runtime provides a unified API for inference across all supported Intel hardware. It also provides advanced capabilities like load balancing across Intel hardware and asynchronous execution. For more information on running inference, refer to the <a href="https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/tree/latest/notebooks/yolov26-optimization" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">YOLO26 notebooks</a>.</p>
<p>Remember, you&#x27;ll need the XML and BIN files as well as any application-specific settings like input size, scale factor for normalization, etc., to correctly set up and use the model with the Runtime.</p>
<p>In your deployment application, you would typically do the following steps:</p>
<ol>
<li>Initialize OpenVINO by creating <code>core = Core()</code>.</li>
<li>Load the model using the <code>core.read_model()</code> method.</li>
<li>Compile the model using the <code>core.compile_model()</code> function.</li>
<li>Prepare the input (image, text, audio, etc.).</li>
<li>Run inference using <code>compiled_model(input_data)</code>.</li>
</ol>
<p>For more detailed steps and code snippets, refer to the <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">OpenVINO documentation</a> or <a href="https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/blob/latest/notebooks/openvino-api/openvino-api.ipynb" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">API tutorial</a>.</p>
<h2 id="openvino-yolo26-benchmarks" class="group relative">OpenVINO YOLO26 Benchmarks<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks">#</a></h2>
<p>The Ultralytics team benchmarked YOLO26 across various model formats and <a href="https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/precision" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">precision</a>, evaluating speed and accuracy on different Intel devices compatible with OpenVINO.</p><section class="admon admon-info"><div class="admon-title"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-info admon-icon" aria-hidden="true"><circle cx="12" cy="12" r="10"></circle><path d="M12 16v-4"></path><path d="M12 8h.01"></path></svg><span>Note</span></div><div class="admon-content"><ul>
<li>
<p>The benchmarking results below are for reference and might vary based on the exact hardware and software configuration of a system, as well as the current workload of the system at the time the benchmarks are run.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>All benchmarks were run with <code>openvino</code> Python package version <a href="https://pypi.org/project/openvino/2026.2.1" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">2026.2.1</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>YOLO26 models on NPU are only supported on Intel® Core™ Ultra™ systems with 2xxV series and 3xx series and above.</p>
</li>
</ul></div></section><h3 id="intel-core-ultra" class="group relative">Intel® Core™ Ultra<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#intel-core-ultra">#</a></h3>
<p>The Intel® Core™ Ultra™ series represents a new benchmark in high-performance computing, engineered to meet the evolving demands of modern users—from gamers and creators to professionals leveraging AI. This next-generation lineup is more than a traditional CPU series; it combines powerful CPU cores, integrated high-performance GPU capabilities, and a dedicated Neural Processing Unit (NPU) within a single chip, offering a unified solution for diverse and intensive computing workloads.</p>
<p>At the heart of the Intel® Core Ultra™ architecture is a hybrid design that enables exceptional performance across traditional processing tasks, GPU-accelerated workloads, and AI-driven operations. The inclusion of the NPU enhances on-device AI inference, enabling faster, more efficient machine learning and data processing across a wide range of applications.</p>
<p>The Core Ultra™ family includes various models tailored for different performance needs, with options ranging from energy-efficient designs to high-power variants marked by the &quot;H&quot; designation—ideal for laptops and compact form factors that demand serious computing power. Across the lineup, users benefit from the synergy of CPU, GPU, and NPU integration, delivering remarkable efficiency, responsiveness, and multitasking capabilities.</p>
<p>As part of Intel&#x27;s ongoing innovation, the Core Ultra™ series sets a new standard for future-ready computing. With multiple models available and more on the horizon, this series underscores Intel&#x27;s commitment to delivering cutting-edge solutions for the next generation of intelligent, AI-enhanced devices.</p>
<p>Benchmarks below run on Intel® Core™ Ultra™ X7 358H, Intel® Core™ Ultra™ 7 258V and Intel® Core™ Ultra™ 7 155H at FP32, FP16 and INT8 precision.</p>

ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load a YOLO26n PyTorch model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.pt&quot;)

# Benchmark YOLO26n speed and accuracy on the COCO128 dataset for all export formats

results = model.benchmark(data=&quot;coco128.yaml&quot;)</code></pre></div></div></div><p>Note that benchmarking results might vary based on the exact hardware and software configuration of a system, as well as the current workload of the system at the time the benchmarks are run. For the most reliable results use a dataset with a large number of images, i.e. <code>data=&#x27;coco.yaml&#x27;</code> (5000 val images).</p></div></section><h2 id="conclusion" class="group relative">Conclusion<a aria-label="Link to this section" class="static before:absolute before:inset-0 ml-2 no-underline opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100 text-muted-foreground hover:text-muted-foreground" href="#conclusion">#</a></h2>
<p>The benchmarking results clearly demonstrate the benefits of exporting the YOLO26 model to the OpenVINO format. Across different models and hardware platforms, the OpenVINO format consistently outperforms other formats in terms of inference speed while maintaining comparable accuracy.</p>
<p>The benchmarks underline the effectiveness of OpenVINO as a tool for deploying deep learning models. By converting models to the OpenVINO format, developers can achieve significant performance improvements, making it easier to deploy these models in real-world applications.</p>
<p>For more detailed information and instructions on using OpenVINO, refer to the <a href="https://docs.openvino.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">official OpenVINO documentation</a>.</p>

ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="code-pre" style="max-height:657.75px"><code>from ultralytics import YOLO

# Load a YOLO26n PyTorch model

model = YOLO(&quot;yolo26n.pt&quot;)

# Export the model

model.export(format=&quot;openvino&quot;)  # creates &#x27;yolo26n_openvino_model/&#x27;</code></pre></div></div></div></div></section><p>For more information, refer to the <a href="/modes/export">export formats documentation</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="what-are-the-benefits-of-using-openvino-with-yolo26-models"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="what-are-the-benefits-of-using-openvino-with-yolo26-models-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">What are the benefits of using OpenVINO with YOLO26 models?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="what-are-the-benefits-of-using-openvino-with-yolo26-models-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Using Intel&#x27;s OpenVINO toolkit with YOLO26 models offers several benefits:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Performance</strong>: Achieve up to 3x speedup on CPU inference and leverage Intel GPUs and NPUs for acceleration.</li>
<li><strong>Model Optimizer</strong>: Convert, optimize, and execute models from popular frameworks like PyTorch, TensorFlow, and ONNX.</li>
<li><strong>Ease of Use</strong>: A large collection of tutorial notebooks is available to help users get started, including ones for YOLO26.</li>
<li><strong>Heterogeneous Execution</strong>: Deploy models on various Intel hardware with a unified API.</li>
</ol>
<p>For detailed performance comparisons, visit our <a href="#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks">benchmarks section</a>.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 Specifically, when combined with OpenVINO, YOLO26 provides:</p>
<ul>
<li>Up to 3x speedup on Intel CPUs</li>
<li>Seamless deployment on Intel GPUs and NPUs</li>
<li>Consistent and comparable accuracy across various export formats</li>
</ul>
<p>For in-depth performance analysis, check our detailed <a href="#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks">YOLO26 benchmarks</a> on different hardware.</p></div></div></div></li><li class="group rounded-lg border bg-card p-4 transition-colors"><h3 class="scroll-mt-24" id="can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino"><button type="button" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino-answer" class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center justify-between gap-3 text-left font-medium">Can I benchmark YOLO26 models on different formats such as PyTorch, ONNX, and OpenVINO?<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down size-4 shrink-0 text-muted-foreground transition-transform duration-150" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></h3><div id="can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino-answer" class="grid transition-[grid-template-rows,opacity,margin] duration-150 ease-out grid-rows-[0fr] opacity-0"><div class="min-h-0 overflow-hidden"><div class="prose-doc"><p>Yes, you can benchmark YOLO26 models in various formats including PyTorch, TorchScript, ONNX, and OpenVINO. Intel OpenVINO Export\n\n\u003cimg width=\"1024\" src=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ultralytics/assets@main/docs/openvino-ecosystem.avif\" alt=\"OpenVINO Intel AI inference toolkit\"\u003e\n\nIn this guide, we cover exporting YOLO26 models to the [OpenVINO](https://docs.openvino.ai/) format, which can provide up to 3x [CPU](https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/cpu-device.html) speedup, as well as accelerating YOLO inference on Intel [GPU](https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/gpu-device.html) and [NPU](https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/openvino-workflow/running-inference/inference-devices-and-modes/npu-device.html) hardware.\n\nOpenVINO, short for Open Visual Inference \u0026 [Neural Network](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/neural-network-nn) Optimization toolkit, is a comprehensive toolkit for optimizing and deploying AI inference models. Even though the name contains Visual, OpenVINO also supports various additional tasks including language, audio, time series, etc.\n\n\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e\n  \u003cbr\u003e\n  \u003ciframe loading=\"lazy\" width=\"720\" height=\"405\" src=\"https://www.youtube.com/embed/AvFh-oTGDaw\"\n    title=\"YouTube video player\" frameborder=\"0\"\n    allow=\"accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share\"\n    allowfullscreen\u003e\n  \u003c/iframe\u003e\n  \u003cbr\u003e\n  \u003cstrong\u003eWatch:\u003c/strong\u003e How to Export Ultralytics YOLO26 to Intel OpenVINO Format for Faster Inference 🚀\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\n## Supported Tasks\n\nOpenVINO export supports all seven Ultralytics tasks. Semantic segmentation and depth estimation are available only with YOLO26, the only family that ships those heads.\n\n| Task                             | [YOLOv8](../models/yolov8.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO11](../models/yolo11.md)                                                                                    | [YOLO26](../models/yolo26.md)                                                                                    |\n| :------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| [Detect](../tasks/detect.md)     | ✅     | ✅     | ✅     |\n| [Segment](../tasks/segment.md)   | ✅   | ✅   | ✅   |\n| [Semantic](../tasks/semantic.md) | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅ |\n| [Depth](../tasks/depth.md)       | ❌                                                                                                               | ❌                                                                                                               | ✅       |\n| [Classify](../tasks/classify.md) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n| [Pose](../tasks/pose.md)         | ✅         | ✅         | ✅         |\n| [OBB](../tasks/obb.md)           | ✅           | ✅           | ✅           |\n\n\n## Usage Examples\n\nThe OpenVINO format supports the [Export](../modes/export.md), [Predict](../modes/predict.md), and [Validate](../modes/val.md) modes. Export your model, then load the exported model to run inference or validate its accuracy on Intel CPU, integrated/discrete GPU, or NPU.\n\n!!! example \"Export\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load a YOLO26 model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.pt\")\n\n        # Export the model to OpenVINO format\n        model.export(format=\"openvino\")  # creates 'yolo26n_openvino_model/'\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Export a YOLO26n PyTorch model to OpenVINO format\n        yolo export model=yolo26n.pt format=openvino # creates 'yolo26n_openvino_model/'\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Predict\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported OpenVINO model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n_openvino_model/\")\n\n        # Run inference\n        results = model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\")\n\n        # Run inference on a specific device, available devices: [\"intel:gpu\", \"intel:npu\", \"intel:cpu\"]\n        results = model(\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\", device=\"intel:gpu\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Run inference with the exported OpenVINO model\n        yolo predict model=yolo26n_openvino_model source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg'\n\n        # Run inference on a specific device, available devices: [\"intel:gpu\", \"intel:npu\", \"intel:cpu\"]\n        yolo predict model=yolo26n_openvino_model source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg' device=\"intel:gpu\"\n        ```\n\n!!! example \"Validate\"\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load the exported OpenVINO model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n_openvino_model/\")\n\n        # Validate accuracy on the COCO8 dataset\n        metrics = model.val(data=\"coco8.yaml\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Validate the exported OpenVINO model\n        yolo val model=yolo26n_openvino_model data=coco8.yaml\n        ```\n\n## Export Arguments\n\n| Argument   | Type             | Default      | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |\n| ---------- | ---------------- | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `format`   | `str`            | `'openvino'` | Target format for the exported model, defining compatibility with various deployment environments.                                                                                                                                                               |\n| `imgsz`    | `int` or `tuple` | `640`        | Desired image size for the model input. Can be an integer for square images or a tuple `(height, width)` for specific dimensions.                                                                                                                                |\n| `quantize` | `int` or `str`   | `None`       | Quantization precision: `16` (FP16) or `8` (INT8/PTQ; needs calibration `data`/`fraction`); `32`/unset is FP32. Replaces the deprecated `half`/`int8` flags.                                                                                                     |\n| `dynamic`  | `bool`           | `False`      | Allows dynamic input sizes, enhancing flexibility in handling varying image dimensions.                                                                                                                                                                          |\n| `nms`      | `bool`           | `False`      | Adds Non-Maximum Suppression (NMS), essential for accurate and efficient detection post-processing.                                                                                                                                                              |\n| `batch`    | `int`            | `1`          | Specifies export model batch inference size or the max number of images the exported model will process concurrently in `predict` mode.                                                                                                                          |\n| `data`     | `str`            | `None`       | Path to the [dataset](../datasets/index.md) YAML, essential for quantization; classification instead takes a dataset directory or a built-in dataset name. If omitted with `quantize=8`, Ultralytics selects the default calibration dataset for the model task. |\n| `fraction` | `float`          | `1.0`        | Specifies the fraction of the dataset to use for INT8 quantization calibration. Allows for calibrating on a subset of the full dataset, useful for experiments or when resources are limited. If not specified with INT8 enabled, the full dataset will be used. |\n\nFor more details about the export process, visit the [Ultralytics documentation page on exporting](../modes/export.md).\n\n!!! warning\n\n    OpenVINO™ is compatible with most Intel® processors but to ensure optimal performance:\n\n    1. Verify OpenVINO™ support\n        Check whether your Intel® chip is officially supported by OpenVINO™ using [Intel's compatibility list](https://docs.openvino.ai/2025/about-openvino/release-notes-openvino/system-requirements.html).\n\n    2. Identify your accelerator\n        Determine if your processor includes an integrated NPU (Neural Processing Unit) or GPU (integrated GPU) by consulting [Intel's hardware guide](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/support/articles/000097597/processors.html).\n\n    3. Install the latest drivers\n        If your chip supports an NPU or GPU but OpenVINO™ isn't detecting it, you may need to install or update the associated drivers. Follow the [driver‑installation instructions](https://medium.com/openvino-toolkit/how-to-run-openvino-on-a-linux-ai-pc-52083ce14a98) to enable full acceleration.\n\n    By following these three steps, you can ensure OpenVINO™ runs optimally on your Intel® hardware.\n\n## Benefits of OpenVINO\n\n1. **Performance**: OpenVINO delivers high-performance inference by utilizing the power of Intel CPUs, integrated and discrete GPUs, and FPGAs.\n2. **Support for Heterogeneous Execution**: OpenVINO provides an API to write once and deploy on any supported Intel hardware (CPU, GPU, FPGA, VPU, etc.).\n3. **Model Optimizer**: OpenVINO provides a Model Optimizer that imports, converts, and optimizes models from popular [deep learning](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/deep-learning-dl) frameworks such as PyTorch, [TensorFlow](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/tensorflow), TensorFlow Lite, Keras, ONNX, PaddlePaddle, and Caffe.\n4. **Ease of Use**: The toolkit comes with a large collection of [tutorial notebooks](https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks) (including [YOLO26 optimization](https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/tree/latest/notebooks/yolov26-optimization)) teaching different aspects of the toolkit.\n\n## OpenVINO Export Structure\n\nWhen you export a model to OpenVINO format, it results in a directory containing the following:\n\n1. **XML file**: Describes the network topology.\n2. **BIN file**: Contains the weights and biases binary data.\n3. **Mapping file**: Holds mapping of original model output tensors to OpenVINO tensor names.\n\nYou can use these files to run inference with the OpenVINO Inference Engine.\n\n## Using OpenVINO Export in Deployment\n\nOnce your model is successfully exported to the OpenVINO format, you have two primary options for running inference:\n\n1. Use the `ultralytics` package, which provides a high-level API and wraps the OpenVINO Runtime.\n\n2. Use the native `openvino` package for more advanced or customized control over inference behavior.\n\n### Inference with Ultralytics\n\nThe ultralytics package allows you to easily run inference using the exported OpenVINO model via the predict method. You can also specify the target device (e.g., `intel:gpu`, `intel:npu`, `intel:cpu`) using the device argument.\n\n```python\nfrom ultralytics import YOLO\n\n# Load the exported OpenVINO model\nov_model = YOLO(\"yolo26n_openvino_model/\")  # the path of your exported OpenVINO model\n# Run inference with the exported model\nov_model.predict(device=\"intel:gpu\")  # specify the device you want to run inference on\n```\n\nThis approach is ideal for fast prototyping or deployment when you don't need full control over the inference pipeline.\n\n### Inference with OpenVINO Runtime\n\nThe OpenVINO Runtime provides a unified API for inference across all supported Intel hardware. It also provides advanced capabilities like load balancing across Intel hardware and asynchronous execution. For more information on running inference, refer to the [YOLO26 notebooks](https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/tree/latest/notebooks/yolov26-optimization).\n\nRemember, you'll need the XML and BIN files as well as any application-specific settings like input size, scale factor for normalization, etc., to correctly set up and use the model with the Runtime.\n\nIn your deployment application, you would typically do the following steps:\n\n1. Initialize OpenVINO by creating `core = Core()`.\n2. Load the model using the `core.read_model()` method.\n3. Compile the model using the `core.compile_model()` function.\n4. Prepare the input (image, text, audio, etc.).\n5. Run inference using `compiled_model(input_data)`.\n\nFor more detailed steps and code snippets, refer to the [OpenVINO documentation](https://docs.openvino.ai/) or [API tutorial](https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino_notebooks/blob/latest/notebooks/openvino-api/openvino-api.ipynb).\n\n## OpenVINO YOLO26 Benchmarks\n\nThe Ultralytics team benchmarked YOLO26 across various model formats and [precision](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/precision), evaluating speed and accuracy on different Intel devices compatible with OpenVINO.\n\n!!! note\n\n    - The benchmarking results below are for reference and might vary based on the exact hardware and software configuration of a system, as well as the current workload of the system at the time the benchmarks are run.\n\n    - All benchmarks were run with `openvino` Python package version [2026.2.1](https://pypi.org/project/openvino/2026.2.1).\n\n    - YOLO26 models on NPU are only supported on Intel® Core™ Ultra™ systems with 2xxV series and 3xx series and above.\n\n### Intel® Core™ Ultra\n\nThe Intel® Core™ Ultra™ series represents a new benchmark in high-performance computing, engineered to meet the evolving demands of modern users—from gamers and creators to professionals leveraging AI. This next-generation lineup is more than a traditional CPU series; it combines powerful CPU cores, integrated high-performance GPU capabilities, and a dedicated Neural Processing Unit (NPU) within a single chip, offering a unified solution for diverse and intensive computing workloads.\n\nAt the heart of the Intel® Core Ultra™ architecture is a hybrid design that enables exceptional performance across traditional processing tasks, GPU-accelerated workloads, and AI-driven operations. The inclusion of the NPU enhances on-device AI inference, enabling faster, more efficient machine learning and data processing across a wide range of applications.\n\nThe Core Ultra™ family includes various models tailored for different performance needs, with options ranging from energy-efficient designs to high-power variants marked by the \"H\" designation—ideal for laptops and compact form factors that demand serious computing power. Across the lineup, users benefit from the synergy of CPU, GPU, and NPU integration, delivering remarkable efficiency, responsiveness, and multitasking capabilities.\n\nAs part of Intel's ongoing innovation, the Core Ultra™ series sets a new standard for future-ready computing. With multiple models available and more on the horizon, this series underscores Intel's commitment to delivering cutting-edge solutions for the next generation of intelligent, AI-enhanced devices.\n\nBenchmarks below run on Intel® Core™ Ultra™ X7 358H, Intel® Core™ Ultra™ 7 258V and Intel® Core™ Ultra™ 7 155H at FP32, FP16 and INT8 precision.\n\n#### Intel® Core™ Ultra™ X7 358H\n\n!!! YOLO26s | PyTorch       | FP32      | ✅      | 19.5      | 0.5703              | 70.92                  |\n            | YOLO26s | OpenVINO      | FP32      | ✅      | 36.7      | 0.5632              | 36.16                  |\n            | YOLO26s | OpenVINO      | FP16      | ✅      | 18.6      | 0.563               | 36.07                  |\n            | YOLO26s | OpenVINO      | INT8      | ✅      | 10.0      | 0.5471              | 15.54                  |\n            | YOLO26m | PyTorch       | FP32      | ✅      | 42.2      | 0.6196              | 192.45                 |\n            | YOLO26m | OpenVINO      | FP32      | ✅      | 78.4      | 0.6191              | 102.52                 |\n            | YOLO26m | OpenVINO      | FP16      | ✅      | 39.5      | 0.618               | 101.88                 |\n            | YOLO26m | OpenVINO      | INT8      | ✅      | 20.6      | 0.6025              | 35.72                  |\n            | YOLO26l | PyTorch       | FP32      | ✅      | 50.7      | 0.6215              | 239.19                 |\n            | YOLO26l | OpenVINO      | FP32      | ✅      | 95.3      | 0.6206              | 129.24                 |\n            | YOLO26l | OpenVINO      | FP16      | ✅      | 48.1      | 0.621               | 128.5                  |\n            | YOLO26l | OpenVINO      | INT8      | ✅      | 25.2      | 0.5984              | 45.38                  |\n            | YOLO26x | PyTorch       | FP32      | ✅      | 113.2     | 0.6512              | 511.89                 |\n            | YOLO26x | OpenVINO      | FP32      | ✅      | 213.2     | 0.6552              | 293.4                  |\n            | YOLO26x | OpenVINO      | FP16      | ✅      | 107.1     | 0.6552              | 296.48                 |\n            | YOLO26x | OpenVINO      | INT8      | ✅      | 54.8      | 0.6418              | 85.24                  |\n\n## Reproduce Our Results\n\nTo reproduce the Ultralytics benchmarks above on all export [formats](../modes/export.md) run this code:\n\n!!! example\n\n    === \"Python\"\n\n        ```python\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\n\n        # Load a YOLO26n PyTorch model\n        model = YOLO(\"yolo26n.pt\")\n\n        # Benchmark YOLO26n speed and accuracy on the COCO128 dataset for all export formats\n        results = model.benchmark(data=\"coco128.yaml\")\n        ```\n\n    === \"CLI\"\n\n        ```bash\n        # Benchmark YOLO26n speed and accuracy on the COCO128 dataset for all export formats\n        yolo benchmark model=yolo26n.pt data=coco128.yaml\n        ```\n\n    Note that benchmarking results might vary based on the exact hardware and software configuration of a system, as well as the current workload of the system at the time the benchmarks are run. For the most reliable results use a dataset with a large number of images, i.e. `data='coco.yaml'` (5000 val images).\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe benchmarking results clearly demonstrate the benefits of exporting the YOLO26 model to the OpenVINO format. Across different models and hardware platforms, the OpenVINO format consistently outperforms other formats in terms of inference speed while maintaining comparable accuracy.\n\nThe benchmarks underline the effectiveness of OpenVINO as a tool for deploying deep learning models. By converting models to the OpenVINO format, developers can achieve significant performance improvements, making it easier to deploy these models in real-world applications.\n\nFor more detailed information and instructions on using OpenVINO, refer to the [official OpenVINO documentation](https://docs.openvino.ai/).\n\n## are the benefits of using OpenVINO with YOLO26 models?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/openvino.md\",\"children\":\"Using Intel's OpenVINO toolkit with YOLO26 models offers several benefits:\\n\\n1. **Performance**: Achieve up to 3x speedup on CPU inference and leverage Intel GPUs and NPUs for acceleration.\\n2. **Model Optimizer**: Convert, optimize, and execute models from popular frameworks like PyTorch, TensorFlow, and ONNX.\\n3. **Ease of Use**: A large collection of tutorial notebooks is available to help users get started, including ones for YOLO26.\\n4. **Heterogeneous Execution**: Deploy models on various Intel hardware with a unified API.\\n\\nFor detailed performance comparisons, visit our [benchmarks section](#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks).\"}]}]\n3f:[\"$\",\"$L3d\",\"how-can-i-run-inference-using-a-yolo26-model-exported-to-openvino\",{\"answerId\":\"how-can-i-run-inference-using-a-yolo26-model-exported-to-openvino-answer\",\"id\":\"how-can-i-run-inference-using-a-yolo26-model-exported-to-openvino\",\"question\":\"How can I run inference using a YOLO26 model exported to OpenVINO?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/openvino.md\",\"children\":\"After exporting a YOLO26n model to OpenVINO format, you can run inference using Python or CLI:\\n\\n!!! example\\n\\n    === \\\"Python\\\"\\n\\n        ```python\\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\n        # Load the exported OpenVINO model\\n        ov_model = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n_openvino_model/\\\")\\n\\n        # Run inference\\n        results = ov_model(\\\"https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg\\\")\\n        ```\\n\\n    === \\\"CLI\\\"\\n\\n        ```bash\\n        # Run inference with the exported model\\n        yolo predict model=yolo26n_openvino_model source='https://ultralytics.com/images/bus.jpg'\\n        ```\\n\\nRefer to our [predict mode documentation](../modes/predict.md) for more details.\"}]}]\n40:[\"$\",\"$L3d\",\"why-should-i-choose-ultralytics-yolo26-over-other-models-for-openvino-export\",{\"answerId\":\"why-should-i-choose-ultralytics-yolo26-over-other-models-for-openvino-export-answer\",\"id\":\"why-should-i-choose-ultralytics-yolo26-over-other-models-for-openvino-export\",\"question\":\"Why should I choose Ultralytics YOLO26 over other models for OpenVINO export?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/openvino.md\",\"children\":\"Ultralytics YOLO26 is optimized for real-time object detection with high accuracy and speed. Specifically, when combined with OpenVINO, YOLO26 provides:\\n\\n- Up to 3x speedup on Intel CPUs\\n- Seamless deployment on Intel GPUs and NPUs\\n- Consistent and comparable accuracy across various export formats\\n\\nFor in-depth performance analysis, check our detailed [YOLO26 benchmarks](#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks) on different hardware.\"}]}]\n41:[\"$\",\"$L3d\",\"can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino\",{\"answerId\":\"can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino-answer\",\"id\":\"can-i-benchmark-yolo26-models-on-different-formats-such-as-pytorch-onnx-and-openvino\",\"question\":\"Can I benchmark YOLO26 models on different formats such as PyTorch, ONNX, and OpenVINO?\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L35\",null,{\"assetBaseUrl\":\"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/main\",\"docsRoot\":\"docs/en\",\"sourcePath\":\"integrations/openvino.md\",\"children\":\"Yes, you can benchmark YOLO26 models in various formats including PyTorch, TorchScript, ONNX, and OpenVINO. Use the following code snippet to run benchmarks on your chosen dataset:\\n\\n!!! example\\n\\n    === \\\"Python\\\"\\n\\n        ```python\\n        from ultralytics import YOLO\\n\\n        # Load a YOLO26n PyTorch model\\n        model = YOLO(\\\"yolo26n.pt\\\")\\n\\n        # Benchmark YOLO26n speed and [accuracy](https://www.ultralytics.com/glossary/accuracy) on the COCO8 dataset for all export formats\\n        results = model.benchmark(data=\\\"coco8.yaml\\\")\\n        ```\\n\\n    === \\\"CLI\\\"\\n\\n        ```bash\\n        # Benchmark YOLO26n speed and accuracy on the COCO8 dataset for all export formats\\n        yolo benchmark model=yolo26n.pt data=coco8.yaml\\n        ```\\n\\nFor detailed benchmark results, refer to our [benchmarks section](#openvino-yolo26-benchmarks) and [export formats](../modes/export.md) documentation.\"}]}]\n45:[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"className\":\"font-medium
